# Hardware rounds

One row per build that went to the phone. The point of this file is so that a
test is never asked for twice: before proposing a run, check whether it is
already here.

**How to use it against me.** If I ask for a hardware run, ask which row it is
not a repeat of. If I state a finding, ask which row established it. If a row's
"settled" column is empty, that round bought nothing and I should say so rather
than let it blur into the next one.

**There are four rules now.** The fourth came after round 93 and it is about
the other half of the problem: the first three stop a *test* being repeated,
and nothing stopped a *guess* being shipped.

> 4. Review your own code before it goes out. Do not just guess, do not just
>    code: read back what you wrote, ask whether it actually makes sense, and
>    check the things you assumed.

`rules.py` runs what can be run for it -- the self-tests, and every hard-coded
ordinal in `gate6.s` against the export table of the ROM that has to answer it
-- and then asks the three questions that cannot be automated: which parts of
this change are guesses and how each could be settled on the bench; which
lines of the diff were read back rather than remembered; and what state the
code can be in that the bench run never entered. All three of the bugs the
first review pass found were in that last category -- a ring that had not
wrapped, a config from the previous build, a key held down.

**The three confirmations are generated, not remembered.**
`toolchain/port/rules.py` prints them from this file -- what is logged, how
many rows a new test has to be checked against, and how far we are with which
round was best. They were given for rounds 47 to 50 and then dropped at round
51 without my noticing. If a reply of mine reports a result or asks for a run
and does not carry them, that is the lapse, not an oversight in the format.

**Both machines are in here.** The emulator table below came late: fifteen
emulator runs happened while only the hardware rounds were being written down,
and they existed only as prose in `PORTING.md`, where a repeat could not be
caught. `emurun.sh` writes each run's row itself now and `checkrec.py` will not
let a stub row survive a commit, so "I forgot to log it" is not available.

The phone is a **Nokia N95** (Symbian 9.2, S60 3rd FP1). The emulator runs an
RM-409 (5320, 9.3 / FP2). Counts are *traced events* unless stated.

**The phone shows two panics per run, and has for many rounds:** one
**KERN-EXEC 0** and one **KERN-EXEC 3**. That was mentioned in rounds up to
about 40 and then stopped being mentioned, and I stopped asking -- so it went
unrecorded through every round since, while every theory in this file was built
around a single failure. KERN-EXEC 3 is an access violation; **KERN-EXEC 0 is a
bad handle**, which is a different fault with a different cause. Both are
kernel-side, so neither is one of our own `G6xxx` panics.

Two panics most likely means two *processes*: the app, and something starting
it again afterwards. The emulator shows exactly that shape -- the run ends with
`User::Leave`, `User::Exit`, and then our loader panics `G6MEM` failing to
allocate the image on a relaunch. **Which of the two the log we read belongs to
is not established**, and it needs to be before the next theory is built on it.

## Emulator runs

Hardware rounds are not the only tests. Most of the work happens here, and the
same rule applies: a run that is not written down gets repeated. `emurun.sh`
appends a row automatically with the record count and the fault, leaving
`TODO` in the last column; `checkrec.py` refuses a commit while any `TODO` is
still there, so the row has to be finished before the next thing is done.

Rows before the script existed were reconstructed from the session that ran
them, and say so.

Every read takes a **TPtr8 with a 65536-byte maximum** (`0x20000000`, `0x10000`), answers **KErrNone**, and comes back with the length word at `0x20010000` -- a full 64 KiB, twenty-six times, then `0x2000007d` (125 bytes) at the end of the file, and two later reads of a 16-byte buffer. `RFile::Size` is called once. So the emulator reads about 1.7 MB in full chunks and stops when the file runs out. **This is the control the phone run needs**: the same four numbers from hardware say whether the short read is a failed call, an empty file, or a descriptor with no room in it |

Five opens, all KErrNone, no allocation ever fails. So the emulator's heap is not the difference and neither is the read: it is the **open of `6rbc.app`** |

Our `CServer::StartL` registers nothing, so the real `CreateSession` could only ever fail. The new stop is honest and small: `Thread Gate6 panicked with G6IMP 464217` -- **import 217, `memmove`**, which 9.x estlib does not export under that name |
| E124 | **build 113 -- supply `memmove` locally** | 285551 | `--` | **The game runs.** `RunL` entered **4,706** times and returned 4,706 times; `CFbsScreenDevice::Update` called **4,716** times. No leave, no exit, no panic -- the run ends because the 120-second timeout kills it, not because the game does. **109,542 core events**, against 388 in E123, 273 in E117 and 180 on build 59. The frame loop is turning over and the screen is being updated about forty times a second. `memmove` was the last thing in the way: the game imports it from the C runtime, 9.x does not export it under that name, and eight instructions of local implementation replaced a panic |

What is drawn is a band across the top of the game area -- roughly 176 by 130 -- of horizontal magenta/green/grey streaks, with the rest of the area white. That is a **pixel format or stride mismatch**, not a content failure: the game is writing a framebuffer in one layout and the screen device is reading it in another. The N-Gage is 176x208 and the RM-409 is 240x320, which is the other half of it. This is the answer to "more than one frame or a black bar with pixels": the loop runs, the screen updates, and what is on it is wrong in a way that is now a display bug rather than a boot failure |

The game ignores the size it is told and writes a linear **176x208 at 16bpp**, which is 73,216 bytes, which is 76 rows of that 960-byte line: exactly the band on the screen, squashed two-to-one and streaked because two of its pixels are being read as one of the emulator's. `CFbsBitmap::Create`, `DataAddress` and `HAL::Get` are never called, so this raw framebuffer is the whole drawing path |

Two things changed at once, which was the mistake: the address handed to the game *and* the size reported to it. Next build changes only the address |

E127's crash was the reported size, not the address: leave `iScreenSize` at the device's 240x320 and the game is happy, even though it draws 176x208 regardless. | E214 | the image scrambled as 6rbc.bin -- the loader unscrambles its own copy; does the game mind reading the scrambled file through the redirect | 29584 | `--` | **It does not, and that is the whole question answered.** 2,401 clocked frames (195 at zero ticks, 1,922 at one, 267 at two, eight at three or more), the audio sequence identical from `Open` through `MaoscOpenComplete(0)`, and the soundtrack opened as usual. Both opens of the image read `6rbc.bin` -- the loader's, which XORs its copy back before parsing, and the game's own, which gets the scrambled bytes and never notices. So the game reads its image but not the first 32 bytes of it, and none of the machinery I had drawn up for the content case -- tracking the file position to patch reads, or writing a clean copy out at first launch -- is needed. The cheap experiment was worth more than the design |
| E215 | build 170 installed into the emulator over an empty tree, then run from what the installer left | 29860 | `--` | **The package is the only source and it works.** The game's directory was deleted first, so every file the run touched came out of the SIS. `Package Asphalt 2 registering with UID: 0xe0001006`, `Installation done!`, and the directory then holds `6rbc.bin` and no `6rbc.app`. | E218 | a third party's "PUpatch" installed over build 171 and run -- what does it actually change | 18721 | `--` | **Two bytes, and they are the two layout constants.** The package is 24 KB, one file, `gate6.exe`, declared **installType 2 -- EInstPartialUpgrade** at version 1.0.8 over v5's 1.0.0, same UID and vendor. It installed over 171 and **all 16 entries of the game tree survived**, which is what that declaration is for. Against v5's executable it differs in exactly two ARM immediates: `0xae80` `moveq r1,#2 -> #0` (`c->mode`, MODE_FILL to MODE_ONE_TO_ONE) and `0xae90` `mov r1,#0x38 -> #0` (`c->topInset`, 56 to 0). | E219 | the zlib uncompress call sites hooked: both callers of 0xd4f88 wrapped so the true status, the buffers and the sizes are recorded before the game flattens every failure to -4 | 32593 | `--` | **The hook fires and every value is sane, which is what it was built to prove.** A `bl` in place of the `bl` at `0x33a74`, into a thunk that records the destination, the room the caller claims is in it, the source, its length and the stream's first word, then calls `0xd4f88` and records the true status and the length written back. Both call sites planted (`0x33a74` and `0x11562c`, the second one found by scanning the image for branches to `0xd4f88`). Twenty-four decompressions recorded: every one begins `0x78 0x9c` -- a zlib stream at default compression -- every status is 0, and `bytes written` equals `room in it` exactly, so the caller knows the uncompressed size in advance and a Z_BUF_ERROR would mean the stored size disagrees with the data. The instrument the third-party report asked for at `0x33a78` would have caught the status alone; hooking the call instead caught the arguments too. Not a repeat of anything -- no earlier row instruments zlib |
| E220 | the other three exits wrapped -- LeaveIfError (logged only when the argument is negative), LeaveNoMemory and Panic -- each with the heap beside it, plus a heap sample every 64 frames | 30109 | `--` | **Three more exits wrapped, and the import that was silently missing.** `LeaveIfError` is called on every result the game gets, so it is filtered in the handler -- a non-negative argument returns before it writes -- which costs a compare on the path it is always on. `LeaveNoMemory` and `Panic` go down whole. The find is procedural: `rheap_available` had been declared in `gate6.s` and used in the handler but never added to `build_gate6.py`'s import list, and the build **did not complain** -- 41 imports before and after. It would have jumped through a null IAT entry on the first failure, which is the exact path it was built for. The import count is the check: 41 -> 42 once it was listed |
| E221 | the heap note made four numbers -- cells and bytes outstanding from User::AllocSize beside the free list and its biggest cell -- because Available alone reads as 2 KB on a growable heap whatever is going on | 30370 | `--` | **`RHeap::Available` is the wrong question, and the right one says 56 MB.** Available reads 2,320 bytes and falls to 1,664 over the run, which on a heap that grows on demand means only that the free list is short. `User::AllocSize` (euser ordinal 664, sitting beside `User::Allocator` at 665, which this port already resolves correctly) answers what is actually held: **18,415 cells / 27.1 MB** through the menus, **27,833 cells / 56.5 MB** once the race starts, and then flat to the end of the run. `gate6.exe` declares `heapMax` 0x4000000 -- 64 MB -- so the bench never notices. A phone has perhaps twenty megabytes to give |
| E222 | the allocation census: every allocator call bucketed by size, the largest kept with its call site, and anything over 64 KB written down -- because 56 MB across 27,833 cells is an average, and an average hides a whale | 30787 | `--` | **772 allocations in the 32-to-64 KB band, and 46 above it.** The histogram over 25,684 allocator calls: 8,349 at 16-32 bytes, 5,631 at 32-64, 3,120 at 128-256, 1,647 at 1-2 KB, 965 at 8-16 KB, **772 at 32-64 KB**, 46 at 64 KB and up. At least 44.7 MB asked for in all against 56.5 MB held, which is the first number saying that essentially nothing is given back. The large sites named themselves: `0xed824` onward is a run of `mov r0, #147456; bl 0xd5a08` -- the game's own 144 KB, 108 KB and 160 KB buffers, hardcoded immediates -- and `0x65324` is inside the game's `operator new`. The band that holds the memory was below the 64 KB threshold and went unnamed, which is E223 |
| E223 | a per-site allocation table beside the histogram: every call site that asks for 8 KB or more, with its running count and total, so the 32-to-64 KB band has a name instead of a size | 33926 | `--` | **One site, 1,492 calls, 32.1 MB.** A per-site table of everything asking for 8 KB or more: `0x1166c0` accounts for 32.1 MB of the 43.9 MB the table sees, `0x65324` (the game's `operator new`) for 8.4 MB, and the game's own big buffers for 3.5 MB between them. `0x1166b0` disassembles to `push {r4,r5,lr}; mul r5,r2,r1; mov r0,r5; bl <alloc>; subs r4,r0,#0; movne r0,r4; movne r1,r5; blne <memset>` -- **`calloc`**, and at 1,492 calls averaging 22 KB it is zlib's `zcalloc`: a 32 KB window and a state block per `inflateInit`, once per decompression. Live cells (26,648) against total allocator calls (25,684) is the whole finding stated another way: **the process frees nothing** |
| E224 | the leak closed: the three deallocators give back anything 4 KB or larger at once and hold everything smaller in a 512-cell quarantine, in place of the no-op that has answered all three since build 49 | 33070 | `--` | **`LEAK_EVERYTHING = 1`, on since build 49, and it is the crashes.** Build 49 answered the three deallocation ordinals with a do-nothing function to test whether the wall it was stuck at was a use-after-free. It advanced, and the switch stayed on, and it has shipped in every build since -- including the one on the user's phone. Replaced with two rules: anything **4 KB or larger goes back at once**, everything smaller into a **512-cell quarantine** returned when that many further frees push it out, so a just-freed small object is still not handed to anyone else. Measured against E223 on the same tree: **26,648 cells / 51.1 MB -> 11,405 cells / 6.5 MB**, an 87% cut; the free list goes from 1,664 bytes to 149,536. The run goes **further**, 2,744 frames against ~2,400, with the audio sequence unchanged (`Open`, `SetAudioPropertiesL`, `MaxVolume`, `SetVolume`, `SetPriority`, `Stop`), the soundtrack opened, no leave, no panic and no access violation. On a phone the leaked 51 MB cannot be committed, and the first allocation that fails is whichever comes next -- in the recorded failure, zlib's own window inside `uncompress`, which returns Z_MEM_ERROR, which `0x33a7c` flattens to -4, which is `User::Leave(-4)` |

| E240 | phase 4: a full-screen mode, and the status-pane inset asked of Avkon (LayoutMetricsRect EMainPane, resolved at run time) instead of the N95's hardcoded 56 | 25947 | `--` | **Avkon answers, and it says 48 where the port assumed 56.** `AknLayoutUtils::LayoutMetricsRect(EMainPane)` resolved at run time through the avkon RLibrary (ordinal 417, to `0x8141a3ed` -- odd, so a Thumb entry, called correctly) and answered a main pane of **(0,48) to (240,293)** on the RM-409. So the status pane is 48 rows here and the softkey pane takes the bottom 27. Adopted: the layout becomes **240x272 at (0,48), 85.0% of the screen, +4.3% aspect error** against 240x264 at (0,56), 82.5% and +7.4%. Bigger *and* less distorted. **This is a deliberate change to the one layout hardware has confirmed**, so round 87 has to look at it: 56 was measured on an N95 (FP1) and 48 is what a 5320 (FP2) reports, and those can legitimately differ -- but if the N95's real band is 56 and Avkon there also says 48, the top eight rows of the picture will sit under the band. Wasting rows is safe; being covered is visible. Three guards keep the worst case at today's behaviour: a null lookup, an implausible rect, or a device that disagrees all fall back to 56, and what was resolved, what it answered and what was adopted are all in the log. Also fixed here: `if (!c->screenW)` guarded only the mode, so the inset was reset on **every** call to screen_info rather than the first -- invisible today, and it would have silently undone the picker's adjustment in phase 5. Full-screen mode added as a fifth option: 100% of every panel at (0,0), -11.4% on an N95 through +168.6% on an E90, and **exactly 0%** on 352x416 and 176x208, which are already 176:208. 2,218 frames, harness gate clean |
| E241 | phase 5: relaunch after the hold test wrote gate6.cfg with mode 4 -- is the choice read back and applied | 18061 | `--` | **The picker works end to end, gesture and persistence both.** Two parts, one experiment. A 2.2-second hold gave **exactly three changes**, aspect -> fill -> integer -> full, at gaps of **+0.50 s and +0.53 s**: one at a second, then one every half second, which is the cadence asked for. Three config writes, all `KErrNone`. The file on disk reads `magic 0x46433647 ('G6CF'), version 1, mode 4, inset 48`. Then this relaunch, with no key touched: **`saved choice found: mode 4, inset override 48`** and the layout comes up **240x320 at (0,0), 100% of the screen, -11.4% aspect** -- full-screen mode, restored. 18,061 records, no fault. The timing is driven from the frame loop rather than key auto-repeat, so it does not depend on whether a given phone's clear key repeats; the key handler notes only the down and the up, and a tap is passed through untouched because the key is swallowed only once a hold has actually changed something |

Also decided here and written up: **the `#` inset key promised in phase 4 is dropped rather than half-delivered** -- `#` is a Chr/Fn symbol on a QWERTY S60v3, so it would work on the numeric phones and not the others, and the mode cycle already spans the cases. That link register is `RAllocator::AllocZ` + 0x18 -- the instruction after its `blx` to euser's memory-clear -- so this is `AllocZ` zeroing the cell `RHeap::Alloc` just returned. **`RHeap::Alloc` returned a pointer into the ROM**, thirty-two bytes of it starting at the `RHeap` vtable, and the first thing that vtable is used for afterwards is the next allocation. So the sequence is: the heap's free list is corrupted long before the crash; an allocation follows the bad link and hands out a ROM address; `AllocZ` clears it and destroys the allocator's own vtable; the next `User::AllocZ` jumps through the hole. The null jump was the third act. This also means the crash site says nothing about the cause, and the emulator's watch is the only thing that can find it -- which is what it now does over a range: `EKA2L1_WATCH=lo:hi`, first forty writes. Third time this week that a per-title collision was found only by trying it, after the import indices, the trace lists and the log paths. The pattern is worth naming: **anything the port writes down once was written down for one game** |

Cancel in between and the completion still arrives, on an object the scheduler no longer holds a request for; every completion signals the thread's request semaphore, so the scheduler wakes with nothing to run. It was written from the constructor alone, without looking at the shim table, and it is the root of three rounds. `CTimer::DoCancel` -- `iTimer.Cancel()` -- was always the right call on the wrapper, and round 99's completion is a *second* completion on top of the kernel timer's: two signals, one stray event, which is E332 exactly |

Frames stop at 200, which is correct: the timer really is cancelled. **So round 99 was a regression and the bench now proves it.** Round 98's DoCancel was the right one and build 016 restores it. The port stubs sysagt whole as no-ops, so the status is never set pending, and the 9.x scheduler finds an object that is active and already complete on every wake -- and runs it first, since 10 beats the game's loop objects at 0 and its CIdle at -20. The game's own loop (the CActive at code+0xb7cfc and the CTimer class at code+0x9fc00) never gets a turn. But the queue after the focus-gained event holds no other game object: the `CIdle` the engine makes at code+0xb5dc0 (priority -20, stored at engine+0xb4) is never started. Its only `Start` site, code+0xb7474, is in the engine's resume routine at 0xb73cc, whose only caller is 0xb5aac -- inside the game's **own `HandleForegroundEventL(TBool)` override** (old app UI slot 7, code+0xb5a8c: `aForeground ? Resume() : Pause()`, then the avkon base through import 15). The wrapper app UI never calls the old UI's overrides except `ConstructL` (old 13): on the Asphalts that was never needed (Asphalt 2 overrides 0, 13, 17; Asphalt 1 overrides 1, 7, 9, 13, 17 and has shipped for a hundred rounds without any of them called). Ashen overrides 0, 7, 9, 13, 17 -- foreground, system event and command -- and starts its game loop from the first. The old slot numbers are read off the vtable itself: 8 is cone `HandleSwitchOnEventL`, 10 eikcore `HandleApplicationSpecificEventL`, 16 `StopDisplayingMenuBar`, so 7 is `HandleForegroundEventL` (9.x 8), 9 `HandleSystemEventL` (9.x 10), 17 `HandleCommandL` (9.x 24). Fix: forward those three from the wrapper's slots to the old UI when the game overrides them (a per-title knob, off for the Asphalts), and map the game's base calls -- avkon 757 `HandleForegroundEventL`, 803 `HandleSystemEventL`, each corroborated by the override that calls it with its own arguments -- onto the wrapper |

Then the host process took SIGSEGV: gdb puts it in EKA2L1's `graphic_context::clear` with no window attached (winuser.cpp:307 via gctx.cpp:680), 9,175 records in both runs, deterministic. Cause: the game drives the 9.x `CWindowGc` by its **old vtable slot numbers** -- word 0xd8 is old entry 52 `Activate(window)` and 9.x slot 54 `Clear()`; word 0xc0 is old `BitBlt(const TPoint&, const CFbsBitmap*)` and 9.x 48 `SetClippingRegion`. Both tables read off the ROMs: the RH-29 ws32 vtable at 0x5058dcc0 (63 entries) named by the wserv opcode each body writes (gcop.def's u139 column: 1 active, 64 clear, 48 blt), and RM-409's `_ZTV9CWindowGc` (ordinal 436) the same way (u151m2 column: 67 clear at 54, 51 blt at 57, 1 active at 68), which also confirms the EABI order computed from GDI.H/W32STD.H by base-class declaration. Fix: the gc the game is handed is a stand-in with the old-shaped table, every entry a thunk into the matching 9.x slot of the real one, as the direct-screen path has done for `CFbsBitGc` since E134 |

The thunk is `ldr r0,[cell]; ldr ip,[cell]; ldr pc,[ip,#slot*4]`: it loads the real object into both registers and indexes the **object**, not its vtable, so slot 68 read word 0x110 of the CWindowGc, which is 0 (r0 = r12 = 0x701440 in the fault frame). After 45 s it opened `demo05.dat`, built the sound thread a second time (two more semaphores, `RThread::Create` from the same site) and took `KERN-EXEC 3` reading 0x1010 in its own code at code+0x8e1e4 (lr 0x8e504): `ldr sb,[r0,#0x34]; cmp sb,#0x1000; movgt sb,#0x1000; ldr sb,[sb,r8,lsl #2]` with r8 = 4, the span height -- a reciprocal-table read whose base is clamped to at most 0x1000, so it always reads the first page of the address space. On the N-Gage that page is mapped; on EKA2 nothing is, so this is a game bug EKA1 forgave and the N95 will not, independent of the port. What the page holds there is the question for the next run. The host SIGSEGV after it is the teardown one again |

Same disease as E369's window gc, third object; the direct-screen path has had the old-shaped `CFbsBitGc` stand-in (kGcSlot) since E134 and this title never goes through that path. The picture is unchanged by the 176x208 control: still a 240-wide, 150-row band of noise (shot40.png), so the frame bitmap is still 240 wide. The control's ConstructL calls `SetRect` after `CreateWindowL`, with the app UI's `ApplicationRect()` -- 240x320 on this ROM -- which undoes the extent the wrapper was given; the N-Gage's ApplicationRect is 176x208. Fix: a per-object `CFbsBitGc` stand-in wrapped around every `CreateContext` result, and ApplicationRect answered as the N-Gage's rect when GAME_CONTROL_W/H are set. 80,267 records |

The two worker faults (g6w0 writing 0x2, g6w1 writing 0x3) are the thread's own exit: after `CActiveScheduler::Start` returns it does `delete cleanup` on the `CTrapCleanup` it made with `CTrapCleanup::New()` -- old ABI, vtable word 2 with r1 = 3 (0xb4e48-0xb4e5c; so GCC98r2's deleting-destructor flag is 3, measured) -- and word 2 of a 9.x CBase vtable is `CBase::Extension_`, which writes its null result through r2 = 3. The same latent call sits in any title's worker that deletes its cleanup stack; the Asphalts' workers never exit. Fix: `CTrapCleanup::New` answered with a stand-in whose old slot 0 forwards to the real deleting destructor, the bitgdi stand-in's delete path reused |

The veneer is `ldr pc,[pc,#-4]` over the word the loader is to patch, and `user_exit` (euser 641) is declared in gate6.s but was never in build_gate6.py's import list, so the word stayed 641 = 0x281 and the branch went to 0x280. The game then handed 0x140 to NewL as its window. On this ROM the measured word was the right one all along; the lesson is to validate every candidate against the session buffer, and to take the buffer from the environment's own Static() rather than a field the port had not filled yet |

Found on the way: the saved fighter is gone at every relaunch -- `6R58.prf` is 12,288 bytes, and at launch the game's loader (image 0x54b1c) meets Z_BUF_ERROR in its zlib (Leave(5) at 0xab3ac) and deletes the file as corrupt (0x5bd98, on -20). The file decodes offline (a word of length, its complement, a 13-byte XOR key from 0x144f98, zlib): valid, and cut mid-stream |
| E584 | One bench: three range probes on the fighter save (CompressL's output, the XORed copy, the RFile::Write descriptor) -- the saved 6R58.prf is cut at 12,288 bytes | 6362 | `--` | **The game hands RFile::Write the whole save**: CompressL answered 12,759 bytes (a complete stream), the XORed copy is 12,767, and the descriptor at RFile::Write is 12,767. The file is 12,288: the tail is lost below the game. A temporary log in EKA2L1's `file_write` (reverted) showed the server write all 12,767 -- and no `file_close` for the file ever came. The game's `RFsBase::Close` was mapped to `RHandleBase::Close`, which closes word 0 of the RFile: the parent session's handle, marked KHandleNoClose in a subsession (us_mes.cpp), so nothing was closed and the host stream was never flushed |
| E585 | One bench: RFsBase::Close mapped to efsrv RFile::Close (300) in gen_shim and the dynamic lookup -- it was RHandleBase::Close, and no file was ever closed; then the fighter save by hand, and a relaunch | 1048 | `--` | **Fixed**: 165 file closes reach the server in one run where there had been none; `6R58.prf` is 12,767 bytes and closed; after a relaunch PLAY goes straight to the play menu and VERSUS shows the saved fighter; the VS fight loads and starts. The slice is never used up: `switch_context` (EKA2L1 scheduler.cpp) refreshes `time` on every switch away from a running thread, so any higher-priority wakeup inside 20 ms (the audio pump, timers, the servers) starts the main thread's slice over. EKA2 refreshes it only when a thread blocks (`TScheduler::Remove`) or is rotated (`RotateReadyList`), `nkern/sched.cpp` -- a preempted thread keeps what it had left |

CBase 66 is the trap bridge's (BUGBOOK E358): its handler table held eight threads (TRAP_THREADS), was never emptied when a thread ended, and matched by address. C3.2's worker was the ninth thread to trap (main + seven workers), got no handler, so its TRAP marked nothing and the first PushL panicked. Any title that makes more than seven trapping threads in a session would die so. Also seen: A3's first copy 7.2 s after its write (not seen again) |

WORKER_LOG back to 0. **The finding of the day is in the emulator**: `kernel_system::close` closes straight on the handle table and never reaches the strict check, so a Close of a bad handle -- freed, stale, or another thread's -- returned an error quietly on the bench under =2, where a device panics KERN-EXEC 0 (sexec.cpp:802). | E776 | One bench, round 143, EKA2L1_DETTICK=10000 sweep: probe count (live 408 / dead 1)? | 13016 | `--` | **13,016 records, 408 probes, 15 chunks -- live, and deterministic.** A 10 ms-per-read virtual clock boots cleanly and reads one.cwa whole. Together with E772-E775 this proves the live/dead outcome is a pure function of the tick the game sees: EKA2L1 serves User::TickCount from the host wall clock (basic_teletimer_micro), so it jitters per launch; a deterministic tick fixes the outcome, and this step fixes it on the live side. (The record count is lower than a wall-clock live run's ~22k because the 10 ms step also paces the rest of the game, so this step is a probe, not a shippable clock.) |

| E821 | One bench, round 143, CYCLETICK=50 traced from the reader construction: the MT seed tick vs the interval stamps. | 12473 | `--` | CYCLETICK=50 traced from the reader's construction (regs at the seed read): **the PRNG seed tick at code+c5d2c is 0x12c**, live. The trace cap cut the rest; the one row is the one that mattered. |
| E822 | One bench, round 143, CYCLETICK=200 traced from the reader construction: the MT seed tick vs the interval stamps. | 9648 | `--` | CYCLETICK=200, same: seed tick **0x4b**, dead. The reader seeds a Mersenne Twister (init_genrand at c58b0: 624-word state at obj+0x9c4, mti 0x270 at +0x9c0) from the hashed TickCount at construction, and the decoded bytes -- including the count of how much more to load -- follow the seed. The interval check (cca10/ccacc) is clean in every run (budget 45 s), the two other construction reads (ab594/ab5e0) discard their result, and FastCounter/NTickCount are never reached (not imported; NTickCount is not in the N-Gage ABI): the seed read is the only tick that enters the data. |

The one TickCount read whose return address is code+c5d2c is answered with 0x12c (the seed the live bench rate gave, E787-E789/E795-E798); every other read is real. One substitution logged, one slot diverted. |

| E833 | One bench, round 143, build 018, audio captured (EKA2L1_AUDIO_CAPTURE_DIR), one fight: which streams carry sound, is there a continuous ambience track? 1 of 1 (full fight, keys mashed): alive (motion>20) or dead (<12)? | 22780 | `--` | Build 018, one fight, audio captured. The fight stream (16 kHz mono, the effects mixer) is **63% silence**: 77 long silent runs, blows as isolated bursts (8.5 onsets per 10 s), mean level 123. The music stream (44.1 kHz stereo) wrote nothing -- the game's music thread never runs on the emulator (E148), a bench gap, not the report. The arena scripts (data/arenas/*.fx: `REPE <period> <weight> <sample>`) say every arena has periodic ambience; none of it is in this capture. |
| E834 | One bench, round 143, build 019 candidate (TLex16::Val(TReal64&) answered in FPA order), audio captured, one fight: do the arena REPE ambience samples now play (bursts between hits), and what did Val parse? 1 of 1 (full fight, keys mashed): alive (motion>20) or dead (<12)? | 23164 | `--` | **Build 019 candidate -- TLex16::Val(TReal64&) answered in FPA word order: the ambience plays.** Same fight, same arena: silence **63% -> 25%**, long silent runs 77 -> 35, mean level 123 -> 319; the gaps between blows are filled (onsets 8.5 -> 4.2 per 10 s because the sounds now overlap). The Math-hook count at install went 9 -> 10 (the Val hook). the first-parse log (NOTE_FPA 0xF1..) reached neither the main nor the worker log, so the parsing runs on a third thread whose records go nowhere (the loading-screen thread, g6w2); the install count and the audio are the evidence. Cause: the arenas' REPE periods and weights are parsed with euser's TLex16::Val(TReal64&), which the 9.x euser writes through the reference in EABI word order; the GCC98r2 game read them high word first, so 4.0 became a denormal near zero and 0.1 a huge negative, and the scheduler never fired. Blows do not go through Val, so the fight had its hits and not its wind. Shipped as One build 019. |
| E835 | One bench, round 145, build 019 + BENCH_MINIMIZE_TICK 3840 / BENCH_RESTORE_TICK 4480, audio captured, one fight: does the arena ambience (REPE samples) go on after the modelled minimize and return, as the N95 says it does not? | 17417 | `--` | The bench's first modelled minimize, confounded: the attack key (5) is also the menu select, so after the restore it walked the pause menu into SETTINGS and the fight never resumed. One fact stands: the worker's stream (stream02, opened at the fight's start) was muted at the minimize and wrote zeros to the end of the run; the main thread's stream came back at the restore with the pause menu's loop. Not a measurement of the report |

The game's volume bookkeeping matched the phone (5A1F: thread 1 wrote on while away, one stream muted at volume 5, no unmute until the next WriteL). Not a measurement of the report either |
| E837 | One bench, round 145, build 019 + BENCH_MINIMIZE_TICK 3840 / BENCH_RESTORE_TICK 4480, audio captured: the modelled minimize at the fight's start, CONTINUE picked from the pause menu (Down Down Return) 4 s after the restore, the fight then played on: does the worker's stream sound again, and the ambience? | 26438 | `--` | **The report reproduced on the bench.** CONTINUE taken (Down Down Return), the fight played on; the worker's stream after the return is blows only -- 45-100% zero samples in every second from s26 on (wavsec.py), against 0-1% with the ambience in E834 -- and the unmute happened (5A1C 0x10000 then 0x10001), so the port's mute is not what silenced it. The game's own file loads after focus gained: loading.noa, then every fighter sample (WeakImpact00 .. youlose) again, and **not one of the arena's** (ship, shipwave0/1, sea, wind10/11, seagull2, which the fight's start had loaded after the fighters'). The N95's round-145 log shows the same reload and the same omission (its arena was wave0/1, seagull1/3) |
| E838 | One bench, round 145, build 019 with the bench knobs off, audio captured: the game's own pause (Return) 12 s into the fight, CONTINUE 17 s later (Down Down Return), the fight played on -- does the arena ambience survive the game's own pause, as a control for the modelled minimize (E837)? | 25925 | `--` | **Control: the game's own pause keeps the ambience.** Pause by key at +43, CONTINUE at +59; the worker's stream is continuous again from s22 on (0-1% zeros, RMS 100-800). The pause menu by itself gives nothing back; the loss is in the focus-lost / focus-gained path |
| E839 | One bench, round 145, build 019 with BG_MUTE 0 and the modelled minimize (3840/4480), audio captured, CONTINUE after the restore: does the arena ambience survive the minimize when the port does not touch the stream's volume? (E837 lost it with the mute on; E838 kept it across the game's own pause) | 26833 | `--` | **Not the mute.** BG_MUTE 0, same modelled minimize and CONTINUE: the ambience is still gone after the return (62-70% zeros per second, blows only). Cause found in the binary instead: the sound manager's focus-lost handler (0x48d54) gives back every decoded sample (0x1216c resets the player's sample array); its focus-gained handler (0x48b70) loads the two fighters' sets again (0x47ffc, 0x481d4) and the arena's (0x483f4) only for entries whose sample id is still -1 -- and the REPE entries (0x64 bytes: TBuf<32> name, type +0x48, period, weight, id +0x5c, last +0x60, in an RArray the manager keeps at +0x58) still hold the ids resolved at the fight's start, which now name slots past the rebuilt array's end. The scheduler (0x458b4, every <period> s at 32768 ticks/s with a Math::FRand draw against the weight) fires into nothing. Fix for E840: set every entry's id back to -1 at focus gained, before the game's handler, so its own reload resolves the arena's set with the fighters' |
| E840 | One bench, round 145, build 020 candidate: ambience_forget (every REPE entry's sample id set to -1 at focus gained, before the game's own reload), the modelled minimize (3840/4480), audio captured, CONTINUE after the restore: are the arena samples loaded again after the return, and does the ambience play on? (E837: lost without it) | 26734 | `--` | **No change, by the port's own mistake:** the hook ran (5A31: a list of 7 entries, the harbour arena's seven REPE lines) and declined, because it read the entry size at RArrayBase +0xc, which in the 9.x euser is iKeyOffset (0); iEntrySize is at +8 (e32cmn.h). Nothing written; the arena's files were not loaded again after the return (the same 42 fighter files as E837), the ambience still gone (45-100% zeros from s27). Fixed for E841. The per-entry guard (TBuf<32> name, type, id) was not reached |
| E841 | One bench, round 145, build 020 candidate, second try: ambience_forget reading iEntrySize at +8 (the 9.x RArrayBase layout; E840 read iKeyOffset and declined), the modelled minimize (3840/4480), audio captured, CONTINUE after the restore: are the arena samples loaded again after the return, and does the ambience play on? | 26715 | `--` | **The ambience survives the minimize.** The hook read a list of 7 entries of 0x64 bytes (5A31 0x6407), set the seven ids to -1 (5A32), and the game's own focus-gained reload then opened the arena's seven files after the fighters' 42 (ship, shipwave1/0, sea, wind10/11, seagull2 -- the first time they came back after a return). After CONTINUE the worker's stream is continuous again: **24% zero samples over s31-100, against 69% without the hook (E837), 73% with the mute off (E839) and 29% across the game's own pause (E838)**. Blows still play (bursts to RMS 900). Build 020 candidate |
| E842 | One bench, round 145, build 020 as it ships but for the bench knobs (3840/4480): ambience_forget then ambience_remember around the game's focus-gained handler, audio captured, CONTINUE after the restore: the arena's seven files loaded again, no 5A34 (nothing left at -1), ambience on after the return? | 26387 | `--` | **Undid the fix, and placed the reload.** With `ambience_remember` putting an id back into every entry the foreground handler returned with at -1, 5A34 said all seven were (the handler itself loads only loading.noa), the arena's files were not opened again and the ambience was gone (68% zero samples s31-100, as E837). In E841 the fighters' and the arena's files came some 1,600 records after `foreground event handled`, with the resume -- the reload is not inside the handler, so the ids have to stay -1 until it. -1 is the state the parser leaves them in until the fight's start resolves them. `ambience_remember` removed; the shipped code is E841's |

| E878 | One bench on the N80 firmware: the LCD channel answers 0x2001 as the ROM's GenericLcd_Lcd.ldd does (a shared 0x1A04-byte update list, the mode, the panel's mode mask), empties the list on 0x2003, and answers 0x2005/0x2006 | 1262 | `0x8` | **StartL returns and the game runs into its first frame: logos, splash, menus and the first sound file are read, and it dies in cone, not bitgdi -- pc 0xf8b72354 (DrawNow entry - 0x785), lr 0xf8b79ce7 (+0x720E), r0 0, reading 8.** 0x2001 now gets what the ROM's own `GenericLcd_Lcd.ldd` gives (its handler at f80b75f8, read for this): a shared 0x1A04-byte list of 0x1A0 dirty rectangles with the count at +0x1A00, the mode it was asked (11, EColor16MU), the panel's mode mask, a flag and four orientation words; a mode outside the mask is KErrNotSupported as there (f80b7508). **The N73's fault box (round 157) has pc 0xf8b602d4, lr 0xf8b67c67 and r12 0xf8b60a59: the same -0x785 and +0x720E from its r12, which is DrawNow's entry here (cone 280, r12 0xf8b72ad9).** So the N73 dies in `CCoeControl::DrawNow`, on the game's old-layout control: 3.0's cone keeps a control's flags behind a pointer at +0x2C (`ldr r0,[this+0x2c]; ldr r0,[r0,#8]`), 3.2's as a plain word at +0x24, and the old object has 0 at +0x2C. Build 027's bitgdi device theory (12.ag) was wrong |

One's Draw slot is a veneer (mask 0x7FFFFFE), so the wrapper runs cone's base Draw -- a blank of the window once, before the first DSA frame, which is what the N-Gage did with the same call. Only One imports DrawNow; the other four tables are unchanged |

| E963 | Colin McRae engine: E951's mailbox stand-in (3 -> 4) with the StartApp hook in | 321 | `0x185B18C` | **E951 again, to the address: the answer goes in (0x9E000003), and the engine thread (renamed COLIN) faults reading 0x185B18C at 0x459e24 with no StartApp before it.** Read this time: 0x459d68 is a block interpreter. Each block opens with a word -- instruction count in the top byte, the next block's offset in the low 24 bits -- and the next block is `[0x560b84+0x14] | offset`, plus 16 MB when below the limit at [0x54023c]; the instructions dispatch on their top three bits through 0x54021c. At the fault the base word reads 0 (r3), so the target is the bare offset 0x85B18C plus 16 MB: a jump into a module whose base was never set. The base's setter exists -- 0x45b0b0, `[0x560b98] = limit & 0xFF000000` -- and nothing in the image reaches it, by branch or pointer: on the N-Gage the engine loaded under 16 MB and 0 was the right base. Next: the port calls 0x45b0b0 once before the engine's thread starts (GAME_ENGINE_INIT) |

StartApp is still never called. Next: input -- whether the menu takes a key |
| E967 | Colin McRae engine as E966, keys on the menu (Right, Right, Left, Return x3) with shots | 297 | `--` | **The menu takes no key: RALLY stays selected through Right, Right, Left and three Returns (32-55 s), and at 65 s the attract demo starts on its own (a snow stage this time), as it does untouched.** The engine reads input itself -- its own RWsSession and RWindowGroup (Construct, SetOrdinalPosition, GetFocusWindowGroup, EventReady/GetEvent, PriorityKeyReady) -- so it hears keys only when its group has the focus; in the port it shares the process with the wrapper app UI, whose group the window server focused first. Next: which group has the focus and gets the keys |
| E968 | Colin McRae engine: the wrapper's root window group declines the focus (EnableReceiptOfFocus(EFalse)), keys on the menu as E967 | 182 | `--` | **The emulator segfaults at once: the last line is the window server's "Request group ... Colin McRae 2005 to disable keyboard focus", then nothing (182 records, no shot, no window to send keys to).** Declined in the app UI's ConstructL, before the engine's thread has made its group, the wrapper's group left no focusable group on the screen, and EKA2L1's window server does not survive that (screen::update_focus leaves the focus null; a phone allows it). Next: decline it only once the engine's group exists -- the I3D block names it in slot 1 -- from the main-thread watcher, which becomes the port's launcher part (GAME_ENGINE_LAUNCHER: the mailbox answer is the launcher's duty, E962) |
| E969 | Colin McRae engine: GAME_ENGINE_LAUNCHER -- the watcher answers the mailbox and, once the engine's group is in slot 1, the wrapper's group declines the focus; keys on the menu as E967 | 298 | `--` | **No crash now -- the init (0xEE45B0B0), the hand-over once slot 1 held the engine's group (0xFE000001; the window server's "disable keyboard focus" on the wrapper's group) and the mailbox answer (0x9E000003) all in -- but the menu still takes no key: RALLY selected through every key, the attract demo at 65 s.** Declining the focus is not enough by itself, or the keys reach the engine and it wants others. Which group the server focuses is not in the record. Next: the watcher samples the focused group and the wrapper's beside slot 1 |
| E970 | Colin McRae engine as E969, the watcher samples the focused window group and the wrapper's beside slot 1; keys as E967 | 344 | `--` | **The engine's group has the focus: every sample reads the focused group 0x14 -- the engine's, in slot 1 -- and the wrapper's 2, with the state 1 and the mailbox 0 (344 records, no fault).** So the hand-over works and the window server routes keys to the engine; the menu still did not move. The harness, read back against E131: `xdotool search --name EKA2L1 | head -1` picks Qt's selection-owner window, which swallows every key -- the key script never reached the emulator in E967-E970. Next: the same build, keys sent to the visible window after windowactivate, as holdtest.sh does |
| E971 | Colin McRae engine as E970, keys sent to the visible emulator window (holdtest.sh's pick) | 346 | `--` | **Colin McRae takes input: Right moves the menu from RALLY to MULTIPLAYER to N-GAGE ARENA, Left back, Return opens MULTIPLAYER -- "Turn Bluetooth ON to start game?" OK/Cancel, then "Bluetooth connection is busy", then Host a Game / Join a Game.** The engine's own menus, driven through its own window group once the wrapper's declines the focus (E969-E970); E967-E970's dead menu was the harness. Next: RALLY, into a stage, with the throttle held |
| E972 | Colin McRae engine as E971: RALLY and Return through the menus, then Up held | 344 | `--` | **Four menus deep on Return alone: RALLY -> MODE SELECT (Championship / Single Rally / Single Stage / Time Trial; Single Stage taken) -> DIFFICULTY (Novice / Intermediate / Expert) -> DRIVER SELECT (Load / Create Driver Profile / Play As Guest) -> TAG ENTRY, `Tag: _ _ _`, where further Returns and a held Up change nothing and only Back is offered.** The tag wants letters. Next: keypad digits for the letters, then on into a stage |
| E973 | Colin McRae engine as E972: digits 2,2,3,4 at the tag entry, then Returns and Up held | 344 | `--` | **The keypad writes the tag -- each 2 gave an `a` (the cursor moved on between presses two seconds apart), 3 gave `d`, the trailing Right and 4 nothing -- `Tag: a a d`, Continue, and Return walks every pre-race screen: CAR SELECT (Subaru Impreza WRX, 300 bhp, 1994 cc, 4WD), TRANSMISSION (Manual / Automatic), COUNTRY SELECT (Finland), STAGE SELECT (one: Mikkeli, 5.0 km, 80% gravel, 20% mud, record 04:51.77), WEATHER SELECT; the held Up cycled the weather list (Random, Drizzle, Cloudy, Bright).** Next: one more Return at the weather, then the stage with a key held |
| E974 | Colin McRae engine as E973, one more Return at the weather, then Up, 5 and Return held in the stage | 400 | `--` | **Past the weather to the service-area screen: QUIT / INFORMATION / SET UP / REPAIR, Stage 1, Mikkeli, time of day 06:00, 5.0 km, 80% gravel 20% mud, 5 C, a map of Finland with Zoom on the left softkey; held 5 toggled the zoomed stage map, and neither a held Up nor a held Return left the screen (67-130 s, no fault).** Next: taps on this screen -- Return, Down, Right -- to find what starts the stage |
| E975 | Colin McRae engine as E974, taps on the service-area screen (Return, Down, Return, Right, Return, Left, Left, Return) then Up held | 379 | `--` | **The service area is tabs: Return and Down toggle the map's zoom, Right goes to SET UP (time remaining 60:00; tyres, gearbox, suspension, power ratio), Return opens TYRES (adjustment 02:00, Gravel-Dry / Mud-Wet / Mud-Dry with the tyre note), Left changes the choice, Return confirms it (time remaining 58:00) and the cursor moves to Gearbox (05:00).** The tab row reads INFORMATION / SET UP / REPAIR / RACE / QUIT. Next: three Rights to RACE and Return |
| E976 | Colin McRae engine as E975: Right x3 to RACE, Return x3, then Up and 5 held | 400 | `--` | **Colin McRae races: RACE and Return load "RALLY FINLAND STAGE 1" (stage record 04:51.77), and the stage runs in 3D -- the start gantry and its lights, the HUD timer counting 00:00.00 to 00:42.64, speed, gear -- and by 42 s the car is out on the stage with the pace-note icons over the road (400 records, no fault; shots/e976-colin-stage-start.png, e976-colin-stage.png).** Held Up and held 5 gave 0-2 MPH: the throttle is some other key, not yet found. Best emulator run of the title so far: the whole single-player path, menu to stage, on the port's own launcher part. Next: the throttle key; then the asphalt2 regression check for the shared shadow-vtable thunk, and the records |
| E977 | Regression: Asphalt 2 on the current shared layer -- CPeriodic::NewL now returns through the shadow-vtable thunk (IMPORT_SHADOW_PERIODIC) | 19772 | `--` | **No regression: 19,772 records (E944's control 18,666), no fault, the attract race at 65 s (lap 1/3, 188 km/h, the Golden Gate) as before.** No 0xCE record: no object Asphalt 2 is handed by CPeriodic::NewL ever went through the shadow -- its frame timer is the port's own timer pair -- so the shared thunk is inert on it. Rule 4's a.: the shadow is exercised only by Colin's front end so far (E958) |
| E978 | Colin McRae engine: EKA2L1_WATCH over all memory narrowed to the key-map store (0x4a6654): the scancode -> engine key table as it is filled | 274 | `--` | **The key map, measured: the table sits at 0x89A024 (object 0x89A020; the arrows' scancodes 0x0E-0x11 land at 0x89A032-35) and is filled three times -- once at boot (lr 0x47a65d8), then twice more from the game script (lr 0x47ceb54, 0x47ceaa0) -- with the same entries but for the digits.** Boot: 2/4/5/6 -> W/A/S/D, 7/8/9/0 and 3 to themselves, 1 -> 3, * -> 5, C -> 4, left soft -> S, right soft -> W, centre -> S. The game's map: 2/3/4 to themselves, 5 -> S, 6 -> D, **7 -> W**, # -> F, the rest as at boot. The engine tracks each key's down/up state (0x4a6660, from EEventKeyDown/Up via 0x4a7658), so a held key stays held. E976 held Up (id 0x10) and 5 (S); W -- on 7 and the right softkey in the game's map -- was never pressed. Next: in the stage, hold 7, F2 (right soft), 2, 8, 1 and # in turn |
| E979 | Colin McRae engine as E976, in the stage: hold 7, F2 (right soft), 2, 8, 1, # in turn (the game's W is on 7 and the right softkey, E978) | 400 | `--` | **7 (the game's W) is brake and reverse: held from the start line the gear goes to R, the gantry recedes, 21 MPH at 2 s, and the car backs into the barrier (000 MPH, R). F2 (right soft, also W in the table) opens PAUSED (EXIT / CONTINUE / GRAPHICS / SOUND) -- the softkey is read before the table, so the table is not the whole input path; held 2 and 8 change nothing there; 1 resumes the stage; # does nothing visible.** Not the throttle, but the reverse of it. Next: from the start line, hold 5, 3, 4, 6, 9, 0, *, F1 and C in turn |
| E980 | Colin McRae engine as E979, from the start line: hold 5, 3, 4, 6, 9, 0, *, F1, C in turn, 3 s each | 400 | `--` | **5 is the throttle: held from the start line, 015 MPH at 1 s and 065 MPH in third at 3 s, the car out on the stage past the first pace notes.** 3 (released into it) let it coast into the bank, 50 MPH in second down to 24; 4, 6, 9, 0 and * moved nothing at a standstill; F1 (left soft) opens PAUSED like F2, and C changes nothing there. 5 is S in both maps, as are the centre key and the left softkey -- the softkeys are taken before the table (E979). E976's "5 gave 0-2 MPH" was a misreading: its shots during that hold show the car carried from the gantry onto the stage, and I read only the speed digits. Next: steering with the throttle held -- the arrows against 4/6 |
| E981 | Colin McRae engine as E980: 5 held from the start, then Left, Right, 4, 6 a second each while it is held | 400 | `--` | **Colin McRae drives: 5 held takes the Impreza to 70 MPH in third by 3 s, Left with it held turns the car left at 82 MPH in fourth (shots/e981-colin-driving.png) and on into the trees at 63, Right brings it back onto the road at 15 MPH; 4 and 6 came with the car pinned against the trees, so they settle nothing.** The controls on the bench: 5 throttle, 7 brake and reverse, the arrows steer, either softkey pauses, 1 resumes from the pause menu. No fault in the run |
| E982 | Colin McRae engine as E981 on the paced audio backend (EKA2L1_AUDIO_CAPTURE_DIR): what each stream plays, menu to stage | 372 | `--` | **The engine plays, but not right: one 16 kHz mono stream (a second, 44.1 kHz stereo, opened and never written), silent to 13 s, then 104 s whose every second looks the same -- RMS about 1,000, peak 3,200, 350 zero crossings -- through menu, loading and racing alike; 97% of the energy under 300 Hz, none above 3 kHz, weak periodicity at 0.091 s.** The samples are smooth and natural (no held runs, 6,550 distinct values), unlike E403's staircase: real material played far too slow, everything pitched down. The pitch step is the suspect, and Colin's GAME_FPA_DOUBLES 1 (doubles to the 9.x helpers word-swapped) was newgame's default, never measured for a PE image from another compiler; the engine holds almost no double constants to tell the order by (one FPA-order 2.0). Next: the same capture with GAME_FPA_DOUBLES 0 |
| E983 | Colin McRae engine on the paced audio backend, GAME_FPA_DOUBLES 0: the menu's audio | 279 | `--` | **No change: the same stream, silent to 10 s, then RMS about 1,000 with 97% of the energy under 300 Hz every 5 s -- doubles are not in the audio path; GAME_FPA_DOUBLES goes back to 1, as measured-shipping since E976.** Read since from the binary and the capture, which corrects E982's reading: the engine opens the stream with an empty package, and in MaoscOpenComplete sets 16000 Hz mono (0x501338: 0x100, 0x2000000; 8000 Hz if that leaves), then feeds it from a 40 ms CPeriodic (0x500f94) -- the stream's format is the engine's own. And the capture is music: its transients fall on a 111 ms grid (111, 222, 333, 444 ms -- sixteenths at about 135 BPM), with pitch-dropping kicks and a bass line under them; bass-heavy, not slowed. Open: the same track runs on into the stage with no engine tone visible, and a second stream (44.1 kHz stereo) is made and never written, against one "Open complete". Next: the engine's whole MDA conversation on RDebug (GAME_MDA_RDEBUG), with the capture |
| E984 | Colin McRae engine, the MDA path on RDebug (GAME_MDA_RDEBUG) with the capture: NewL, every stream call, every callback | 281 | `--` | **No G6N, G6M or G6K at all: Colin's engine never goes through the port's MDA proxy.** Its NewL resolves straight to the 9.x CMdaAudioOutputStream, whose vtable agrees with the old one from slot 3 (kMdaMap's note), so Open, SetAudioPropertiesL(16000, mono) and the writes go to the real stream directly. The engine runs its own sound thread (0x5009d8 creates it, priority 30; 0x50114c builds a scheduler and the audio object). The empty 44.1 kHz stereo stream00 is in every title's captures (Ashen's too): not Colin's. Same music capture as E982-E983. Next: whether effects are mixed in -- throttle on 8 s, off 8 s, on 8 s in a stage, against the capture |
| E985 | Colin McRae engine, capture on: in the stage, 5 held 8 s, released 8 s, held 8 s -- are effects in the mix | 400 | `--` | **Effects are in the mix: in both throttle windows (capture 111.0-118.6 s and 126.8-134.9 s) the 1.2-4 kHz band rises from 65 to 160-560 and the low bands move, and off the throttle it drops straight back to the music-only baseline (about 400 / 315 / 145 / 65).** So the engine's own thread mixes music and the engine note into its 16 kHz stream and feeds it. One thing does not add up: the engine's callback vtable (0x53d7b8, at object +4) reads as GCC 2.x -- offset word, then the three Maosc entries from +8 -- while a 9.x stream (and EKA2L1's, impl.cpp) calls MaoscOpenComplete through slot 0, and Colin has no patch for it. Next: a PC trace over the engine's MaoscOpenComplete and its set-up, to see whether it runs |
| E986 | Colin McRae engine, EKA2L1_PCTRACE over 0x501300-0x501860: do MaoscOpenComplete (0x50178c) and the post-open set-up (0x501304) run | 253 | `--` | **The callback interface works: MaoscOpenComplete (0x50178c) runs once, the post-open set-up (0x501304) once and on its 16 kHz branch (0x50134c, no fallback to 8 kHz), and MaoscBufferCopied (0x501810) 141 times with KErrNone in about 30 s of stream; MaoscPlayComplete never.** So the reading of the callback vtable in E985 was wrong somewhere (the engine's entries are where a 9.x stream looks for them); the measurement settles it. With E982-E985: Colin's sound works on the bench as the engine ships it -- its own thread, NewL straight to the 9.x stream, 16 kHz mono, music with the engine note mixed in -- and needs nothing from the port. GAME_MDA_RDEBUG goes back to 0. Not settled: a phone |
| E987 | Colin McRae on the N80 firmware (RM-92, S60 3.0, 352x416), first run: shots and the audio capture | 309 | `--` | **Colin McRae runs on S60 3.0 as on 3.2: the Codemasters splash at 20 s, the main menu (briefly "v 3.06") from 35 s to the end, 309 records, no fault; sound the same 16 kHz mono stream from 13 s (RMS about 1,000, the same spectrum as E982-E984), dropping to RMS 160 at 80 s.** The picture sits in the 352x416 panel's fit with black borders. The first Colin run on any firmware but the RM-409. Next: input and a stage on the N80 -- the focus hand-over and the keys on 3.0 |
| E988 | Colin McRae on the N80 firmware: the E981 drive (menus, tag, stage, 5 held and steering), 6 s later for the slower boot | 358 | `--` | **Input works on 3.0: the whole pre-race path walks on the same keys -- service area (Stage 3, Joensuu, 2.0 km: the stage the save on C: has reached), SET UP, REPAIR, RACE, "LOADING RALLY FINLAND STAGE 3" -- but the load runs from 96 s to past 129 s, the bar at about 90% when the throttle went down, against about 15 s on the RM-409 (E976).** No fault. The throttle and steering landed on the loading screen, so they say nothing. Next: the same with the stage part after the load |
| E989 | Colin McRae on the N80 firmware as E988, throttle and steering at 160 s, after the slow load | 358 | `--` | **Colin drives on S60 3.0: the stage starts after the long load, and 5 with the arrows takes the Impreza to 70 MPH in third, 83 in fourth, round the bends with the pace notes, a white frame and a reset at 13 s (an off, the game's recovery), then on again to 70 (shots m_160-m_171).** 358 records, no fault. So on the N80 firmware Colin runs as on the RM-409 -- menus, keys, focus hand-over, stage, sound (E987) -- slower to load (about 40 s against 15). Next: the package, installed the way a phone installs it |
| E990 | E990 (manual, runinstalled.sh): Colin McRae installed the way a phone installs it -- build_release.py's package, built from a copy of the game tree with no 6r66.lxe (build_release now inflates it from 6r66.nax, and refuses a .lxe in the tree that is not that inflation), installed with the emulator's installer (--install) on an E: and C: emptied of the title, then run as installed, no emurun copy | 302 | `--` | **The package installs and plays from nothing: 28 files land in E:\system\apps\6r66 (6r66.lxe the generated one, 6r66.bin the scrambled stub), the loader, its resources and icon; the run shows the main menu at 25-45 s and the attract demo at 70 s, music from 10 s, 302 records, no fault, with no save on C:.** Before this the release shipped the engine image only because a bench run had left 6r66.lxe in the tree; from a card dump it would have shipped none, and the loader would have found nothing to run. Also seen: two builds of the same tree are not byte-identical (they differ at byte 21), whatever game_files' docstring says -- package contents, not package bytes, are what can be compared |
| E991 | Colin McRae: hold C (BackSpace) 4 s on the main menu -- does the picture-mode picker step | 260 | `--` | **The picker is dead on Colin: C held 4 s on the main menu, the picture unchanged at 32, 34, 36 and 39 s, and no gate6-6r66.cfg written.** As read beforehand: the picker hears C through the wrapper's key handlers (hold_key), and since E969 the wrapper's group has declined the focus to the engine's, which reads its own keys. A regression against the rule every title keeps (CLAUDE.md, hold C). Next: hold_key fed from the engine's own RWsSession::GetEvent (old ws32 118), the hold's clock the main thread's 100 ms timer, the save deferred to it |
| E992 | Colin McRae: hold C read from the engine's own GetEvent (gate6_engine_get_event), C held 4 s on the main menu | 303 | `--` | **Hold C works on Colin again: the 4 s hold steps the picture six times -- modes 2, 3, 4, 0, 1, 2, the first at 1 s and then every half second, as hold_tick paces it -- the shots show the fit change (smaller at 34 s, filling at 36 s), and the release saves mode 2 once (NOTE_CFG_WROTE 0x20000: mode 2, KErrNone) to C:\gate6-6r66.cfg.** The keys come from the engine's GetEvent on its thread, the steps and the save from the main thread's hold timer. Next: whether the saved mode is read back at the next launch, and the main menu's way out |
| E993 | Colin McRae: the main menu walked to the right (7 Rights) to find its way out; the hold's saved mode 2 read back at launch? | 273 | `--` | **The saved mode comes back: the menu opens in E992's mode 2 (the fill, taller than the default) with no key touched. And the main menu is RALLY / MULTIPLAYER / N-GAGE ARENA / OPTIONS / CREDITS / QUIT / DRIVERS, round in a ring -- QUIT is five Rights from RALLY.** 273 records, no fault. Next: QUIT, and how the process ends |
| E994 | Colin McRae: QUIT from the main menu (5 Rights, Return, Return) -- how the process ends | 301 | `--` | **QUIT asks first: "Are you sure?" Yes / No, with No selected, so the second Return went back to the main menu (shots q2_37-48). 301 records, no fault.** Next: QUIT, Up to Yes, Return |
| E995 | Colin McRae: QUIT from the main menu, Yes -- how the process ends | 308 | `--` | **QUIT then Yes gives a white screen and a process that never ends: the engine and main thread trade time to the timeout, and the block's mailbox reads 6 from the moment of Yes (samples 9f000001 6 14 0 0 14 2).** The binary: QUIT's path is 0x449344 -- 0x4492e8 posts 6 and spins until the launcher clears the word (no timeout), then 0x44926c posts 1 and waits up to 8 s for a clear. The port, as launcher, answered neither. (0x44926c alone is also called from three script opcodes; 0x4492e8 from one more.) Next: the launcher part clears 6 and remembers it, and on a 1 after a 6 ends the process cleanly, as a launcher closing the game would |
| E996 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, with the launcher part answering 6 (cleared) and the 1 after it (the process exits) | 260 | `--` | **The launcher's answer takes (0x9E000006: the 6 cleared), and the engine goes on to shut down -- it kills its sound thread (g6w0, exit 0) -- and then its own thread dies KERN-EXEC 3: an access violation reading 0x4 at 0x4a8044, GAME_CODE_PATCHES' "delete the CDirectScreenAccess" (slot +4) on an object whose vtable word is 0.** The 1 never came, so 0xE81E is not in the record; the process ended only because the emulator ends one whose thread panics, then segfaulted in its known teardown. The object is the port's old-layout stand-in for the DSA (dsaShadow, handed to the game by the NewL thunk), which had no vtable at all: no title had deleted it before -- the Asphalts leave through User::Exit. On a phone the engine thread would die and leave the app up. Next: the stand-in gets a vtable whose every slot deletes the real CDirectScreenAccess and forgets it |
| E997 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, with the DSA stand-in given a deleting vtable (gate6_dsa_shadow_delete) | 260 | `--` | **Past the DSA: the stand-in's delete goes through (no fault at 0x4a8044), and the engine's thread dies one step later -- an access violation writing 0x0 in euser's CBase::Extension_, reached from CActive::Extension_, called from the display object's destructor (0x4a7168) after the delete at 0x4a7194.** The binary: that delete is the engine's message-server observer (+0x5c, vtable 0x53d93c), whose destructor (0x538ca0) deletes its CMsvSession -- a ROM CActive from CMsvSession::OpenSyncL -- through GCC 2.x's slot +8, CActive::Extension_ on 9.x: E957's bug on a factory the shadow list did not have. The same destructor deletes the ROM CWsScreenDevice (+4) so, and the startup deletes a CApaWindowGroupName (0x4a6fdc) so. The DE7E record is missing for E961's reason (the engine thread's records never flush). Next: those factories shadowed too, behind a check that a shadowed class's slot +8 really is CBase's or CActive's Extension_ |
| E998 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, with CMsvSession, CWsScreenDevice, CApaWindowGroupName (and the engine's other factories) shadowed, the shadow refusing any class whose slot +8 is not an Extension_ | 260 | `--` | **The same fault, to the register: writing 0x0 in CBase::Extension_ from CActive::Extension_, the return into the display object's destructor at 0x4a7198.** No 0xCE or 0xCF record either way -- and none could appear: the factories run on the engine's thread (E961), so whether any shadow was made is not in the record. Next: the shadow says what it did on RDebug (G6C) |
| E999 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, as E998 with the shadow on RDebug (G6C/G6v/G6w) | 260 | `--` | **The shadow refused three classes and made one: G6C00cf for vtables 0x80a56c1c (apgrfx), 0x804b7da4 (ws32) and 0x80443644 -- the CMsvSession the fault comes through (r3 in E997) -- and G6C0001 for CPeriodic (0x801caaa0); the fault unchanged.** The ROM says why: each refused class's slot +8 is its DLL's own veneer, `ldr pc, [pc, #-4]` then 0x801a4eaf (CBase::Extension_) or 0x801a0fb5 (CActive::Extension_); only euser's own CPeriodic points at the function itself. The check compared addresses and was too strict. Next: it looks through one such veneer |
| E1000 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, the shadow's Extension_ check looking through a DLL's veneer | 309 | `--` | **The teardown gets through: four classes shadowed (G6C0001-0004: the apgrfx, ws32 and msgs ones and CPeriodic), no access violation, the engine kills its sound thread, writes cmr_save_info.dat and ends its own thread cleanly -- after it only the main thread is scheduled, and the block reads state 5 with slot 1 cleared (9f000005 ... 0 ... 0).** But the process stays up: the engine never posted the 1 my close path waited for. State 5 is the block's "everyone exits" (the front end quits on it). Next: the launcher part exits on state 5 as well |
| E1001 | Colin McRae: QUIT, Yes, the launcher part exiting on the block's state 5 | 261 | `--` | **QUIT works: Yes, the launcher clears the 6 (0x9E000006), the engine tears down -- sound thread killed, save written, no fault -- and sets the block to state 5, and the launcher part ends the game (0xE81E): the main thread exits with 0, the process goes and takes the engine's thread with it ("COLIN forcefully killed ... Domino"), then the emulator's known teardown segfault.** Three port fixes made it: the launcher's answers (6, state 5), the DSA stand-in's deleting vtable, and the shadow list widened with its Extension_ check. Next: regressions -- One (now with a CFileMan shadow) and Asphalt 2 (the DSA stand-in's vtable), and Colin's drive |
| E1002 | Regression: One on the current shared layer (CFileMan shadowed with the Extension_ check; the DSA stand-in's deleting vtable) | 11255 | `--` | **Clean: 11,255 records in 70 s (E875's regression of build 027: 10,561), no access violation, no panic; one class shadowed (G6C0001), so the Extension_ check passed one of One's factories.** Next: Asphalt 2 |
| E1003 | Regression: Asphalt 2 on the current shared layer (the DSA stand-in's deleting vtable, the shadow's Extension_ check, the engine-only hooks absent) | 19071 | `--` | **Clean: 19,071 records in 70 s (E977: 19,772), no access violation, no panic, no shadow made (as in E977).** Next: Colin's drive on the new layer |

| E1008 | Colin McRae: the bench app switch -- another app's group in front at 40 s, the wrapper's group brought back at 50 s; keys at 56 and 59 | 260 | `--` | **Void as a test of the port: the bench's other app took the focus (0x5B010000; the next sample's focused group 0x1b, not the engine's 0x14), and then the main thread panicked CONE 46, the emulator halting on a corrupted graphics command list.** CONE 46 is ECoePanicInvalidHandle in CCoeRedrawer::RunL (cone COEPRIV.CPP:72): the bench made its window on cone's own window-server session, so cone's redrawer got that window's redraw and took its handle (0xB0B1) for a control. A phone's other app is another process on another session. Next: the bench's other app on a session of its own |
| E1009 | Colin McRae: the bench app switch with the other app on its own session -- in front at 40 s, the wrapper's group brought back at 50 s; keys at 56 and 59 | 310 | `--` | **The switch as a phone does it, and the game does not come back: at 40 s the other app takes the focus (0x5B010000; focused group 0x1b), and after the wrapper's group is brought back at 50 s (0x5B020000) the focus stays 0x1b for good, the screen black under a "Colin McRae 2005" title and clock, keys going to the other app.** Predicted from the design: apparc brings an application back by its registered group, the wrapper's, which declines the focus since E969, and the engine's group stays behind the other application. On a phone: invisible and deaf after the first app switch. Next: the launcher part hands the front to the engine's group when the wrapper's is in front and the focus is elsewhere |
| E1010 | Colin McRae: the bench app switch with the launcher's hand-back (the engine's group to the front when the wrapper's comes back) | 311 | `--` | **The hand-back works and the picture does not: at the wrapper's return (0x5B020000) the launcher moves the engine's group to the front at once (0x6B000014), the focus is the engine's again in every sample after, and the title bar is gone -- but the screen stays black to the end, through two Rights.** Whether the engine stopped drawing or its frames stopped reaching the screen (its direct screen access was aborted when the other app covered it) is not in the record. Next: the engine's frame count in the watcher's samples |
| E1011 | Colin McRae: the bench switch with the hand-back, the engine's frame count sampled (0x7F) | 328 | `--` | **The engine never stops drawing: its frame count climbs about 200 per sample (4 s, 50 a second) before, during and after the switch (0x7F00007a ... 0x7F000afd), with the hand-back at the return.** So its frames stop reaching the screen: each post goes through gate6_screen_update, which refuses frames while the screen is lost (an abort with no restart) or the region empty. Next: the post path's state in the samples |
| E1012 | Colin McRae: the bench switch, the post path's state sampled (0x8F: lost, idle, clip; refused posts; reached flags) | 379 | `--` | **The region empties and stays empty: the clip mode is 1 (the whole screen) before the switch, 0 (nothing) from the moment the other app covers the engine, and still 0 after the hand-back, with every post refused from then (awayUpd 207 ... 1449); the screen is never marked lost and no abort or restart reaches the port's observer (reached 0).** EKA2L1's window server does abort a running DSA when its visible region changes (dsa.cpp: visible_region_changed); the observer runs on the engine's thread, where the record cannot see it. Next: the abort, restart and StartL on RDebug |
| E1013 | Colin McRae: the bench switch, the engine's DSA abort/restart/StartL on RDebug (G6A/G6R/G6S) | 379 | `--` | **No abort, no restart, two StartL calls -- both at startup (G6S0001, and G6S0002 beside the sound thread's set-up), none at or after the switch.** So the engine never starts its DSA again once covered: its region, read client-side by dsa_box, stays empty, and the port's restart path would in any case leave the DSA nobody's until a StartL the engine never makes. On a phone, where the Restart does come, the screen would stay black the same way. Next: the launcher's hand-back has the engine's next post start the real DSA again, on the engine's thread |
| E1014 | Colin McRae: the bench switch with the hand-back and the DSA kick (the engine's next post starts its DSA again) | 379 | `--` | **The kick fires at the hand-back and finds the DSA still active (G6K00ac), so it does not start it; the region stays empty and every post is refused to the end (awayUpd to 1,402), the screen black.** The request is pending -- no abort completed it -- though the region emptied. Next: CActive::Cancel (euser 1088) on the real DSA first, then StartL |
| E1015 | Colin McRae: the bench switch, the DSA kick cancelling the pending request before its StartL | 379 | `--` | **The picture and the keys come back after the switch: at the hand-back the kick cancels the pending request and starts the DSA again (G6K0001), the clip mode returns to 1 and refused posts stop at 512; the screenshots after the return show the main menu, and two Rights move the selection RALLY -> MULTIPLAYER -> N-GAGE ARENA.** The app-switch return works on the bench. Best test so far for backgrounding on engine titles. Next: set GAME_BENCH_SWITCH_AT back to 0, regressions |
| E1016 | Colin McRae: the phone's DSA abort and restart on the engine's thread at post 1500 (bench knob), the restart's kick | 376 | `--` | **The engine's own Restart starts its DSA: at post 1500 the knob ends the request, then AbortNow and Restart run through the port's observer (G6A0001, G6R0001), and the engine's StartL follows at once (G6S0002); the kick stays unset (G6B0000), the clip stays 1, no post is refused, and the screenshots after it show the menu, two Rights moving to N-GAGE ARENA.** So E1013's reading -- that the engine starts its DSA only while it sets up -- was wrong: it does on a Restart, the documented pattern, and the bench never sent one. The slot1 kick added for that guess is taken out. On a phone the hand-back's kick can now land between ws32's RunL and the engine's CIdle Restart, two StartLs on one object (SetActive twice). Next: reproduce that order on the bench |
| E1017 | Colin McRae: the abort with the hand-back's kick between RunL and the engine's Restart (bench knob), no guard | 376 | `--` | **The race is real: the kick starts the DSA between the abort and the restart (G6K0001), the engine's Restart then calls StartL on the active object (G6S0002), and from there the engine calls StartL on every frame to the end (G6S0003 ... G6S05bc).** On a phone that second StartL is fatal: wserv's CWsDirectScreenAccess::Request panics the client EWservPanicDirectMisuse for a session already running (nonnga Direct.CPP). The bench case fails without a guard, so it can test one. Next: gate6_dsa_startl skips the real StartL while the DSA is active |
| E1018 | Colin McRae: the abort with the kick between RunL and Restart (bench knob), StartL guarded while active | 376 | `--` | **Guarded, the same order is harmless: the kick starts the DSA (G6K0001), the engine's Restart StartL finds it active and is answered without a second Request (G6D0002), and there are no further StartL calls; the clip stays 1, no post is refused, the menu shows throughout and two Rights move RALLY -> MULTIPLAYER -> N-GAGE ARENA.** The fix for E1017. Next: the app switch again with the guard and the once-per-arrival hand-back |

| E1031 | Colin McRae build 002 with EKA2L1_STRICTHANDLE=1: the handles the emulator rejects (round 161's g6eng KERN-EXEC 0) | 310 | `--` | **Found on the bench: the engine thread uses three handles the emulator rejects -- 0x403e0020 twice (pc 0x8019dc9c, lr 0x801a5f51) and 0x403a000c (pc 0x8019e38c, lr 0x801a8a5b), thread-local handles (bit 30) of the main thread -- each beside the port's own screen-setup notes on that thread: NOTE_SCREEN, NOTE_PANE_FN (status_pane_off: CAknAppUi::SetFullScreenApp on the wrapper UI) and NOTE_INSET_FN (avkon_inset: AknLayoutUtils::LayoutMetricsRect).** The engine asks for its screen on its own thread, and the port's handler (firstTime in the screen-info path) calls Avkon there, through the main thread's window-server session. A phone panics the thread KERN-EXEC 0 at the first one: round 161. Next: STRICTHANDLE=2 on this build, to see the bench reproduce the phone |

Next: Avkon's calls made on the main thread before the engine's thread starts, none from the engine's |
| E1033 | Colin McRae: Avkon, the inset and the picture-mode file read on the main thread before the engine starts (round 161's fix), EKA2L1_STRICTHANDLE=2 | 337 | `--` | **The fix gets the engine going under the phone's handle rules: no bad handle at start, the focus handed (0xFE000001), frames climbing (0x7F00007d ... 0x7F00078f), the menu at 36-56 s; Avkon answered on the main thread (SetFullScreenApp and LayoutMetricsRect found; main pane 0,48 - 240,293).** But at the first key (Right at 56 s) the engine thread (COLIN: g6eng renamed itself) closes a handle that is not one, 0x2e725035 (pc 0x8019df5c, lr 0x801a7ffb), and is panicked KERN-EXEC 0; the run ends a second later. The lenient bench never showed it; a phone would kill the game at the first key on the menu. Next: STRICTHANDLE=1 with the same keys, to count the closes per key |
| E1034 | Colin McRae: the round-161 fix with EKA2L1_STRICTHANDLE=1 and two Rights on the menu: the engine's bad close per key | 370 | `--` | **Not one close but forty-two: after the first Right the engine thread (COLIN) closes a bad handle again and again -- 0x3673001a, 0x36735a27, 0x36742663, ... 0x36b56076 -- each larger than the last by 20,000-50,000, a microsecond clock read where the engine keeps a handle, not a handle at all.** None before the key (E1031, E1033). A phone panics at the first (E1033). Next: the engine's RHandleBase::Close call sites, and which object's word it passes |
| E1035 | Colin McRae: RHandleBase::Close's veneer traced with registers (lr: the engine's caller), STRICTHANDLE=1, two Rights | 370 | `--` | **Named: 154 of the 162 closes come from one engine function, 0x4a6b60 -- a one-word RSystemAgent on its stack, RSystemAgent::RSystemAgent() (sysagt 18), Connect (20), GetState(TUid) (2), then RHandleBase::Close on it (0x4a6b90).** The System Agent is gone on 9.x and the shim answers its whole library with a no-op returning 0, so the constructor never zeroes the handle: the word is whatever the stack held -- 0 before the key, a clock value after it -- and Close hands that to the kernel. Colin's front end and Ashen import the same constructor. Next: the constructor answered as RHandleBase's (iHandle = 0; LOCAL_HANDLE_CTOR), so the Close is the null-handle no-op |
| E1036 | Colin McRae: round 161's two fixes (Avkon/cfg on the main thread; RSystemAgent's constructor zeroes its handle), STRICTHANDLE=2, two Rights | 370 | `--` | **Clean under the phone's handle rules: no bad handle in the whole run, no panic; the menu at 36 s, and after the Rights the engine carries on into its attract demo (LOADING RALLY KENYA STAGE 3 at 59 s) to the end, 370 records.** The two fixes together: the engine starts (E1033) and survives keys (E1034's 42 closes gone).
